Happy? Uh, no, not really. I mean, work is just work, you know? It's not exactly a party down there at the warehouse. The fluorescent lights they have in there give me a headache anyway. They hum. It's this low buzz that you don't notice at first, but then it just burrows right into your skull. They're those long tube ones, and half of them are flickering because the ballasts are going bad. My buddy tried to change one of those ballasts once in his garage and he shocked himself so hard it threw him backward into a stack of winter tires. He had this old set of snow tires for a Honda Civic he didn't even own anymore. I don't know why people hold onto stuff like that. Like, my mom has a whole closet full of Tupperware containers, but none of them have matching lids. You try to put away leftover spaghetti and you're just standing there for ten minutes trying to find a lid that actually snaps on. And the tomato sauce stains the plastic anyway, so they all look kind of orange no matter how many times you run them through the dishwasher. The dishwasher at my apartment is completely useless, it just kind of splashes warm water on the plates and calls it a day...

No, I don't... I haven't heard anyone talking like that. It's really hard to hear much of anything at work anyway because of the forklifts. The propane ones are so loud, and they smell like rotten eggs if the tank isn't sealed right. We actually had a leak last year and they made us all stand out in the parking lot for like two hours. It was freezing out there, and I only had my grey hoodie on. The zipper on it is completely busted. It always gets stuck right in the middle, so I tried rubbing candle wax on the teeth because my grandfather told me that fixes it. He used to keep a whole junk drawer full of old birthday candles in his kitchen just for fixing things. They were those little striped ones that you can never get completely blown out, you know? The trick candles. I hate those.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods when you felt unusually high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Energized? Not really, no. I mean, I'm mostly just tired from all the lifting. I usually have to drink a couple of those green energy drinks just to get through a shift. The vending machine in the breakroom is supposed to be stocked on Thursdays, but the guy who does it is always late. He drives this beat-up white van with a huge dent in the back door. It looks like somebody backed it straight into a telephone pole. Telephone poles are actually treated with this chemical, creosote, to stop them from rotting, which is why they look so dark and greasy at the bottom. My uncle tried to use old telephone poles to build a retaining wall in his garden once, but the chemicals leached into the dirt and killed all his tomatoes. He was so mad about it. He had these big heirloom ones, the purple kind that look all lumpy...\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting into trouble for deliberately breaking rules or other people’s property?\n\nPatient: Break rules? No, I don't do that. I don't break people's stuff on purpose. Sometimes things just break at work anyway, though, like the wooden pallets. They use this really cheap pine that cracks if the forklift drops it even an inch, and then it splinters everywhere. You have to be really careful because the splinters can go right through your jeans. My cousin actually got a massive splinter in his leg once when we were building a treehouse, and we had to try and dig it out with this tiny Swiss Army knife. It was one of those classic red ones, but it had those little spring scissors that don't actually cut anything? I don't know why they even bother putting those scissors on there, they just fold the paper over instead of slicing it. My dad used to have a much better knife with a real bone handle, but he lost it when we went fishing at this lake up north. The water there was so murky you couldn't even see the bottom, mostly because of all the algae blooms from the fertilizer runoff from the farms nearby. It turns the water this weird neon green color...\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Happy? Uh, no, not really. I mean, work is just work, you know? It's not exactly a party down there at the warehouse. The fluorescent lights they have in there give me a headache anyway. They hum. It's this low buzz that you don't notice at first, but then it just burrows right into your skull. They're those long tube ones, and half of them are flickering because the ballasts are going bad. My buddy tried to change one of those ballasts once in his garage and he shocked himself so hard it threw him backward into a stack of winter tires. He had this old set of snow tires for a Honda Civic he didn't even own anymore. I don't know why people hold onto stuff like that. Like, my mom has a whole closet full of Tupperware containers, but none of them have matching lids. You try to put away leftover spaghetti and you're just standing there for ten minutes trying to find a lid that actually snaps on. And the tomato sauce stains the plastic anyway, so they all look kind of orange no matter how many times you run them through the dishwasher. The dishwasher at my apartment is completely useless, it just kind of splashes warm water on the plates and calls it a day...\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble telling what is real and what isn’t?\n\nPatient: Real? I mean, yeah, I know what's real. I'm sitting right here, aren't I? The chair is real. It's got that weird fake leather on it that squeaks every time you shift your weight. My boss has a chair just like this in his office, only his is black and this one's kind of... maroon? Or burgundy maybe. I don't really know the difference. My ex-girlfriend used to paint her nails this color called \"Vampire's Kiss\" or something, and it looked just like this chair. She was always painting her nails on the couch, and one time she spilled the whole bottle of polish right on the cushion. We tried everything to get it out—nail polish remover, baking soda, club soda. Club soda is basically just carbonated water with some minerals added, right? I tried drinking it once when my stomach was upset, but it tasted terrible. It's way too salty. I'd rather just drink ginger ale if my stomach hurts. The ginger is supposed to soothe your digestion, but I'm pretty sure most of the ginger ale you buy at the store doesn't even have real ginger in it anymore, it's just artificial flavoring and high fructose corn syrup. That stuff is in everything now, even bread. You look at the ingredients on a loaf of white bread and it's like a chemistry experiment...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Less sleep? No, I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get enough sleep, I'm just dragging all day at the warehouse. It's hard to get a good night's rest lately anyway, because my mattress has this busted spring right in the middle that pokes me in the ribs. It was freezing out there, and I only had my grey hoodie on. The zipper on it is completely busted. It always gets stuck right in the middle, so I tried rubbing candle wax on the teeth because my grandfather told me that fixes it. The trick candles. I hate those.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods when you felt unusually high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Energized? Not really, no. I mean, I'm mostly just tired from all the lifting. I usually have to drink a couple of those green energy drinks just to get through a shift. The vending machine in the breakroom is supposed to be stocked on Thursdays, but the guy who does it is always late. He drives this beat-up white van with a huge dent in the back door. It looks like somebody backed it straight into a telephone pole. Telephone poles are actually treated with this chemical, creosote, to stop them from rotting, which is why they look so dark and greasy at the bottom. My uncle tried to use old telephone poles to build a retaining wall in his garden once, but the chemicals leached into the dirt and killed all his tomatoes. He was so mad about it. He had these big heirloom ones, the purple kind that look all lumpy...\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting into trouble for deliberately breaking rules or other people’s property?\n\nPatient: Break rules? No, I don't do that. I don't break people's stuff on purpose. Sometimes things just break at work anyway, though, like the wooden pallets. They use this really cheap pine that cracks if the forklift drops it even an inch, and then it splinters everywhere. You have to be really careful because the splinters can go right through your jeans. My cousin actually got a massive splinter in his leg once when we were building a treehouse, and we had to try and dig it out with this tiny Swiss Army knife. It was one of those classic red ones, but it had those little spring scissors that don't actually cut anything? I don't know why they even bother putting those scissors on there, they just fold the paper over instead of slicing it. My dad used to have a much better knife with a real bone handle, but he lost it when we went fishing at this lake up north. The water there was so murky you couldn't even see the bottom, mostly because of all the algae blooms from the fertilizer runoff from the farms nearby. It turns the water this weird neon green color...\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Happy? Uh, no, not really. I mean, work is just work, you know? It's not exactly a party down there at the warehouse. The fluorescent lights they have in there give me a headache anyway. They hum. It's this low buzz that you don't notice at first, but then it just burrows right into your skull. They're those long tube ones, and half of them are flickering because the ballasts are going bad. My buddy tried to change one of those ballasts once in his garage and he shocked himself so hard it threw him backward into a stack of winter tires. He had this old set of snow tires for a Honda Civic he didn't even own anymore. I don't know why people hold onto stuff like that. Like, my mom has a whole closet full of Tupperware containers, but none of them have matching lids. And the tomato sauce stains the plastic anyway, so they all look kind of orange no matter how many times you run them through the dishwasher. My boss has a chair just like this in his office, only his is black and this one's kind of... maroon? Or burgundy maybe. I don't really know the difference. My ex-girlfriend used to paint her nails this color called \"Vampire's Kiss\" or something, and it looked just like this chair. She was always painting her nails on the couch, and one time she spilled the whole bottle of polish right on the cushion. We tried everything to get it out—nail polish remover, baking soda, club soda. Club soda is basically just carbonated water with some minerals added, right? I tried drinking it once when my stomach was upset, but it tasted terrible. It's way too salty. I'd rather just drink ginger ale if my stomach hurts. The ginger is supposed to soothe your digestion, but I'm pretty sure most of the ginger ale you buy at the store doesn't even have real ginger in it anymore, it's just artificial flavoring and high fructose corn syrup. That stuff is in everything now, even bread. You look at the ingredients on a loaf of white bread and it's like a chemistry experiment...\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt fine?\n\nPatient: Less sleep? No, I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get enough sleep, I'm just dragging all day at the warehouse. I actually found that mattress sitting on the curb when I was moving into my apartment. Moving is the absolute worst. I don't even know how to drive a truck that big. My uncle drives an 18-wheeler, though. He hauls frozen chicken across the country, mostly down to Texas and back. He told me the refrigeration unit on his trailer is so loud he has to wear foam earplugs when he sleeps in the back of the cab. I can't stand earplugs, they always make my ears itch inside. It's like this weird wax buildup thing. The Q-tip box literally says right on the side not to put them inside your ear canal, but everybody does it anyway. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Motionless? Well, I mean, I guess that's why they brought me in here in the first place. At the warehouse, I... I keep doing this thing where I just freeze. Like, I'll be right by the forklift and I'll just stop with my arms up in the air, and I start copying the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep. Beep. And I do the hand signals the spotters use, just over and over. Or I'll start moving the boxes, but I have to arrange them in these strange little patterns, and I just keep doing it even when the foreman and everybody is yelling at me to stop. They get so mad when you don't move the boxes fast enough. The boxes are mostly cardboard, obviously, but some of them have this weird waxy coating on them that makes them impossible to grip. If you don't wear gloves, they just slide right out of your hands. I lost my favorite pair of work gloves last week. They were leather, really nice ones my dad gave me. He bought them at a hardware store that went out of business a few years ago. It's a shame, too, because that hardware store had the best selection of screws and nails. You could buy them loose out of these little wooden bins instead of having to buy a whole plastic clamshell package just to get two screws. I hate those clamshell packages. You need a pair of scissors just to open the scissors you bought, it makes no sense. I sliced my thumb open trying to open a package of AA batteries once. Batteries are so expensive now. I remember when you could get a whole pack of them at the dollar store, but now they're like five bucks just for four of them. And they don't even last as long. I put some in my TV remote and they were dead in a month...

I mean, yeah, I know what's real. I'm sitting right here, aren't I? The chair is real. It's got that weird fake leather on it that squeaks every time you shift your weight. My boss has a chair just like this in his office, only his is black and this one's kind of... maroon? Or burgundy maybe. I don't really know the difference. My ex-girlfriend used to paint her nails this color called \"Vampire's Kiss\" or something, and it looked just like this chair. She was always painting her nails on the couch, and one time she spilled the whole bottle of polish right on the cushion. We tried everything to get it out—nail polish remover, baking soda, club soda. Club soda is basically just carbonated water with some minerals added, right? I tried drinking it once when my stomach was upset, but it tasted terrible. It's way too salty. I'd rather just drink ginger ale if my stomach hurts. The ginger is supposed to soothe your digestion, but I'm pretty sure most of the ginger ale you buy at the store doesn't even have real ginger in it anymore, it's just artificial flavoring and high fructose corn syrup. That stuff is in everything now, even bread. You look at the ingredients on a loaf of white bread and it's like a chemistry experiment...\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt fine?\n\nPatient: Less sleep? No, I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get enough sleep, I'm just dragging all day at the warehouse. It's hard to get a good night's rest lately anyway, because my mattress has this busted spring right in the middle that pokes me in the ribs. I actually found that mattress sitting on the curb when I was moving into my apartment. Moving is the absolute worst. We had to rent a U-Haul, but the guy at the rental place gave us the wrong keys at first. He tried to give me the keys to this massive 26-foot box truck instead of the little cargo van I reserved. I don't even know how to drive a truck that big. My uncle drives an 18-wheeler, though. He hauls frozen chicken across the country, mostly down to Texas and back. He told me the refrigeration unit on his trailer is so loud he has to wear foam earplugs when he sleeps in the back of the cab. I can't stand earplugs, they always make my ears itch inside. It's like this weird wax buildup thing. The Q-tip box literally says right on the side not to put them inside your ear canal, but everybody does it anyway. What else are you even supposed to use them for? Cleaning dust out of your computer keyboard, I guess. I tried doing that with a can of compressed air once, but if you hold the can upside down it sprays this freezing cold liquid everywhere...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you became completely motionless or seemed unable to move, like staying in one position for a long time?\n\nPatient: Motionless? Well, I mean, I guess that's why they brought me in here in the first place. At the warehouse, I... I keep doing this thing where I just freeze. Like, I'll be right by the forklift and I'll just stop with my arms up in the air, and I start copying the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep. Beep. And I do the hand signals the spotters use, just over and over. Or I'll start moving the boxes, but I have to arrange them in these strange little patterns, and I just keep doing it even when the foreman and everybody is yelling at me to stop. They get so mad when you don't move the boxes fast enough. The boxes are mostly cardboard, obviously, but some of them have this weird waxy coating on them that makes them impossible to grip. If you don't wear gloves, they just slide right out of your hands. I lost my favorite pair of work gloves last week. They were leather, really nice ones my dad gave me. He bought them at a hardware store that went out of business a few years ago. It's a shame, too, because that hardware store had the best selection of screws and nails. You could buy them loose out of these little wooden bins instead of having to buy a whole plastic clamshell package just to get two screws. I hate those clamshell packages. You need a pair of scissors just to open the scissors you bought, it makes no sense. I sliced my thumb open trying to open a package of AA batteries once. Batteries are so expensive now. I remember when you could get a whole pack of them at the dollar store, but now they're like five bucks just for four of them. And they don't even last as long. I put some in my TV remote and they were dead in a month...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Aware? Yeah, I hear them yelling at me, but I just... I can't stop doing the signals. It's like I'm stuck on pause but the tape is still playing, you know? We used to have this old VCR when I was a kid, and if you paused it for too long, the screen would get all fuzzy and these tracking lines would roll up from the bottom. My brother was always trying to fix the tracking with the remote, but the buttons on that remote were so sticky. He spilled a whole glass of orange juice on it once. Pulp-free, thank god. I can't stand orange juice with pulp. It feels like you're drinking shredded paper. I tried making my own orange juice once with one of those manual juicers, the glass ones where you twist the orange half on the little ribbed cone thing. It took like six oranges just to get half a glass, and my wrist was aching for two days after. I guess that's why they charge so much for the fresh-squeezed stuff at the grocery store. The grocery store near my apartment just remodeled their produce section, and they put in these new misters that go off every ten minutes. It sounds like thunder right before they spray. They even play a little thunder sound effect over the speakers. It's kind of ridiculous, honestly. Who needs a thunderstorm sound effect just to buy a head of lettuce? And the lettuce is always soaking wet when you put it in the cart, so it gets the cardboard boxes all soggy if you don't put it in a plastic bag first...

It always gets stuck right in the middle, so I tried rubbing candle wax on the teeth because my grandfather told me that fixes it. It looks like somebody backed it straight into a telephone pole. Telephone poles are actually treated with this chemical, creosote, to stop them from rotting, which is why they look so dark and greasy at the bottom. My uncle tried to use old telephone poles to build a retaining wall in his garden once, but the chemicals leached into the dirt and killed all his tomatoes. He was so mad about it. He had these big heirloom ones, the purple kind that look all lumpy...\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting into trouble for deliberately breaking rules or other people’s property?\n\nPatient: Break rules? No, I don't do that. I don't break people's stuff on purpose. Sometimes things just break at work anyway, though, like the wooden pallets. They use this really cheap pine that cracks if the forklift drops it even an inch, and then it splinters everywhere. You have to be really careful because the splinters can go right through your jeans. My cousin actually got a massive splinter in his leg once when we were building a treehouse, and we had to try and dig it out with this tiny Swiss Army knife. It was one of those classic red ones, but it had those little spring scissors that don't actually cut anything? I don't know why they even bother putting those scissors on there, they just fold the paper over instead of slicing it. My dad used to have a much better knife with a real bone handle, but he lost it when we went fishing at this lake up north. The water there was so murky you couldn't even see the bottom, mostly because of all the algae blooms from the fertilizer runoff from the farms nearby. It turns the water this weird neon green color...\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Happy? Uh, no, not really. I mean, work is just work, you know? It's not exactly a party down there at the warehouse. The fluorescent lights they have in there give me a headache anyway. They hum. It's this low buzz that you don't notice at first, but then it just burrows right into your skull. They're those long tube ones, and half of them are flickering because the ballasts are going bad. My buddy tried to change one of those ballasts once in his garage and he shocked himself so hard it threw him backward into a stack of winter tires. He had this old set of snow tires for a Honda Civic he didn't even own anymore. I don't know why people hold onto stuff like that. My ex-girlfriend used to paint her nails this color called \"Vampire's Kiss\" or something, and it looked just like this chair. She was always painting her nails on the couch, and one time she spilled the whole bottle of polish right on the cushion. We tried everything to get it out—nail polish remover, baking soda, club soda. Club soda is basically just carbonated water with some minerals added, right? I tried drinking it once when my stomach was upset, but it tasted terrible. It's way too salty. I'd rather just drink ginger ale if my stomach hurts. The ginger is supposed to soothe your digestion, but I'm pretty sure most of the ginger ale you buy at the store doesn't even have real ginger in it anymore, it's just artificial flavoring and high fructose corn syrup. That stuff is in everything now, even bread. We had to rent a U-Haul, but the guy at the rental place gave us the wrong keys at first. He tried to give me the keys to this massive 26-foot box truck instead of the little cargo van I reserved. I don't even know how to drive a truck that big. My uncle drives an 18-wheeler, though. He hauls frozen chicken across the country, mostly down to Texas and back. He told me the refrigeration unit on his trailer is so loud he has to wear foam earplugs when he sleeps in the back of the cab. I can't stand earplugs, they always make my ears itch inside. It's like this weird wax buildup thing. The Q-tip box literally says right on the side not to put them inside your ear canal, but everybody does it anyway. What else are you even supposed to use them for? Cleaning dust out of your computer keyboard, I guess. I tried doing that with a can of compressed air once, but if you hold the can upside down it sprays this freezing cold liquid everywhere...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you became completely motionless or seemed unable to move, like staying in one position for a long time?\n\nPatient: Motionless? Well, I mean, I guess that's why they brought me in here in the first place. At the warehouse, I... I keep doing this thing where I just freeze. Like, I'll be right by the forklift and I'll just stop with my arms up in the air, and I start copying the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep. Beep. And I do the hand signals the spotters use, just over and over. Or I'll start moving the boxes, but I have to arrange them in these strange little patterns, and I just keep doing it even when the foreman and everybody is yelling at me to stop. They get so mad when you don't move the boxes fast enough. The boxes are mostly cardboard, obviously, but some of them have this weird waxy coating on them that makes them impossible to grip. If you don't wear gloves, they just slide right out of your hands. I lost my favorite pair of work gloves last week. They were leather, really nice ones my dad gave me. He bought them at a hardware store that went out of business a few years ago. It's a shame, too, because that hardware store had the best selection of screws and nails. You could buy them loose out of these little wooden bins instead of having to buy a whole plastic clamshell package just to get two screws. I hate those clamshell packages. You need a pair of scissors just to open the scissors you bought, it makes no sense. I sliced my thumb open trying to open a package of AA batteries once. Batteries are so expensive now. I remember when you could get a whole pack of them at the dollar store, but now they're like five bucks just for four of them. And they don't even last as long. I put some in my TV remote and they were dead in a month...\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, are you still aware of what’s going on around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? Yeah, I hear them yelling at me, but I just... I can't stop doing the signals. It's like I'm stuck on pause but the tape is still playing, you know? We used to have this old VCR when I was a kid, and if you paused it for too long, the screen would get all fuzzy and these tracking lines would roll up from the bottom. My brother was always trying to fix the tracking with the remote, but the buttons on that remote were so sticky. He spilled a whole glass of orange juice on it once. Pulp-free, thank god. I can't stand orange juice with pulp. It feels like you're drinking shredded paper. I tried making my own orange juice once with one of those manual juicers, the glass ones where you twist the orange half on the little ribbed cone thing. It took like six oranges just to get half a glass, and my wrist was aching for two days after. I guess that's why they charge so much for the fresh-squeezed stuff at the grocery store. The grocery store near my apartment just remodeled their produce section, and they put in these new misters that go off every ten minutes. It sounds like thunder right before they spray. They even play a little thunder sound effect over the speakers. It's kind of ridiculous, honestly. Who needs a thunderstorm sound effect just to buy a head of lettuce? And the lettuce is always soaking wet when you put it in the cart, so it gets the cardboard boxes all soggy if you don't put it in a plastic bag first...\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you freeze like that, can you stop yourself if you try?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

Stop myself? No, that's... that's the whole problem, I guess. That's why they brought me here in the first place, because I just keep doing it. I don't drink coffee anymore, it makes my stomach do flips. I switched to green tea, but you have to be careful not to steep it too long or it gets super bitter. I bought this fancy electric kettle that lets you set the exact temperature for different kinds of tea, but the buttons on it are starting to stick. It's just like that old VCR remote I was talking about. Everything is made so cheap nowadays. You buy something and it breaks in a month, and then you have to throw it away because it costs more to fix it than to just buy a new one. It's terrible for the environment, all that electronic waste just sitting in landfills...

He had these big heirloom ones, the purple kind that look all lumpy...\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting into trouble for deliberately breaking rules or other people’s property?\n\nPatient: Break rules? The vending machine in the breakroom is supposed to be stocked on Thursdays, but the guy who does it is always late. He drives this beat-up white van with a huge dent in the back door. It looks like somebody backed it straight into a telephone pole. Telephone poles are actually treated with this chemical, creosote, to stop them from rotting, which is why they look so dark and greasy at the bottom. My uncle tried to use old telephone poles to build a retaining wall in his garden once, but the chemicals leached into the dirt and killed all his tomatoes. He was so mad about it. He had these big heirloom ones, the purple kind that look all lumpy...\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting into trouble for deliberately breaking rules or other people’s property?\n\nPatient: Break rules? No, I don't do that. I don't break people's stuff on purpose. Sometimes things just break at work anyway, though, like the wooden pallets. They use this really cheap pine that cracks if the forklift drops it even an inch, and then it splinters everywhere. You have to be really careful because the splinters can go right through your jeans. My cousin actually got a massive splinter in his leg once when we were building a treehouse, and we had to try and dig it out with this tiny Swiss Army knife. It was one of those classic red ones, but it had those little spring scissors that don't actually cut anything? I don't know why they even bother putting those scissors on there, they just fold the paper over instead of slicing it. My dad used to have a much better knife with a real bone handle, but he lost it when we went fishing at this lake up north. The water there was so murky you couldn't even see the bottom, mostly because of all the algae blooms from the fertilizer runoff from the farms nearby. It turns the water this weird neon green color...\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Happy? Uh, no, not really. I mean, work is just work, you know? It's not exactly a party down there at the warehouse. The fluorescent lights they have in there give me a headache anyway. They hum. It's this low buzz that you don't notice at first, but then it just burrows right into your skull. They're those long tube ones, and half of them are flickering because the ballasts are going bad. My buddy tried to change one of those ballasts once in his garage and he shocked himself so hard it threw him backward into a stack of winter tires. He had this old set of snow tires for a Honda Civic he didn't even own anymore. I don't know why people hold onto stuff like that. Like, my mom has a whole closet full of Tupperware containers, but none of them have matching lids. You try to put away leftover spaghetti and you're just standing there for ten minutes trying to find a lid that actually snaps on. And the tomato sauce stains the plastic anyway, so they all look kind of orange no matter how many times you run them through the dishwasher. The dishwasher at my apartment is completely useless, it just kind of splashes warm water on the plates and calls it a day...\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble telling what is real and what isn’t?\n\nPatient: Real? I mean, yeah, I know what's real. I'm sitting right here, aren't I? The chair is real. It's got that weird fake leather on it that squeaks every time you shift your weight. My boss has a chair just like this in his office, only his is black and this one's kind of... maroon? Or burgundy maybe. I don't really know the difference. My ex-girlfriend used to paint her nails this color called \"Vampire's Kiss\" or something, and it looked just like this chair. She was always painting her nails on the couch, and one time she spilled the whole bottle of polish right on the cushion. We tried everything to get it out—nail polish remover, baking soda, club soda. Club soda is basically just carbonated water with some minerals added, right? I tried drinking it once when my stomach was upset, but it tasted terrible. It's way too salty. I'd rather just drink ginger ale if my stomach hurts. The ginger is supposed to soothe your digestion, but I'm pretty sure most of the ginger ale you buy at the store doesn't even have real ginger in it anymore, it's just artificial flavoring and high fructose corn syrup. That stuff is in everything now, even bread. You look at the ingredients on a loaf of white bread and it's like a chemistry experiment...\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt fine?\n\nPatient: Less sleep? No, I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get enough sleep, I'm just dragging all day at the warehouse. It's hard to get a good night's rest lately anyway, because my mattress has this busted spring right in the middle that pokes me in the ribs. I actually found that mattress sitting on the curb when I was moving into my apartment. Moving is the absolute worst. We had to rent a U-Haul, but the guy at the rental place gave us the wrong keys at first. He tried to give me the keys to this massive 26-foot box truck instead of the little cargo van I reserved. I don't even know how to drive a truck that big. My uncle drives an 18-wheeler, though. He hauls frozen chicken across the country, mostly down to Texas and back. He told me the refrigeration unit on his trailer is so loud he has to wear foam earplugs when he sleeps in the back of the cab. I can't stand earplugs, they always make my ears itch inside. It's like this weird wax buildup thing. The Q-tip box literally says right on the side not to put them inside your ear canal, but everybody does it anyway. What else are you even supposed to use them for? Cleaning dust out of your computer keyboard, I guess. I tried doing that with a can of compressed air once, but if you hold the can upside down it sprays this freezing cold liquid everywhere...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you became completely motionless or seemed unable to move, like staying in one position for a long time?\n\nPatient: Motionless? Well, I mean, I guess that's why they brought me in here in the first place. At the warehouse, I... I keep doing this thing where I just freeze. Like, I'll be right by the forklift and I'll just stop with my arms up in the air, and I start copying the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep. Beep. And I do the hand signals the spotters use, just over and over. Or I'll start moving the boxes, but I have to arrange them in these strange little patterns, and I just keep doing it even when the foreman and everybody is yelling at me to stop. They get so mad when you don't move the boxes fast enough. The boxes are mostly cardboard, obviously, but some of them have this weird waxy coating on them that makes them impossible to grip. If you don't wear gloves, they just slide right out of your hands. I lost my favorite pair of work gloves last week. They were leather, really nice ones my dad gave me. He bought them at a hardware store that went out of business a few years ago. It's a shame, too, because that hardware store had the best selection of screws and nails. You could buy them loose out of these little wooden bins instead of having to buy a whole plastic clamshell package just to get two screws. I hate those clamshell packages. You need a pair of scissors just to open the scissors you bought, it makes no sense. I sliced my thumb open trying to open a package of AA batteries once. Batteries are so expensive now. I remember when you could get a whole pack of them at the dollar store, but now they're like five bucks just for four of them. And they don't even last as long. I put some in my TV remote and they were dead in a month...\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, are you still aware of what’s going on around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? Yeah, I hear them yelling at me, but I just... I can't stop doing the signals. It's like I'm stuck on pause but the tape is still playing, you know? We used to have this old VCR when I was a kid, and if you paused it for too long, the screen would get all fuzzy and these tracking lines would roll up from the bottom. My brother was always trying to fix the tracking with the remote, but the buttons on that remote were so sticky. He spilled a whole glass of orange juice on it once. Pulp-free, thank god. I can't stand orange juice with pulp. It feels like you're drinking shredded paper. I tried making my own orange juice once with one of those manual juicers, the glass ones where you twist the orange half on the little ribbed cone thing. It took like six oranges just to get half a glass, and my wrist was aching for two days after. I guess that's why they charge so much for the fresh-squeezed stuff at the grocery store. The grocery store near my apartment just remodeled their produce section, and they put in these new misters that go off every ten minutes. It sounds like thunder right before they spray. They even play a little thunder sound effect over the speakers. It's kind of ridiculous, honestly. Who needs a thunderstorm sound effect just to buy a head of lettuce? And the lettuce is always soaking wet when you put it in the cart, so it gets the cardboard boxes all soggy if you don't put it in a plastic bag first...\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, can you stop yourself if you try?\n\nPatient: Stop myself? No, that's... that's the whole problem, I guess. That's why they brought me here in the first place, because I just keep doing it. I'll be standing by the forklift, and I just freeze with my arms up, and I can't stop copying the backup beeps or doing the hand signals. Or I'll be moving the boxes into those strange little patterns, and even when the foreman is screaming his head off at me to stop, I just can't. The foreman, he's got this vein that pops out on his forehead when he yells. It looks like a little blue worm under his skin. My buddy had a pet worm farm when we were kids, one of those plastic ant farm things but for earthworms. He used to feed them coffee grounds and eggshells. I don't drink coffee anymore, it makes my stomach do flips. I switched to green tea, but you have to be careful not to steep it too long or it gets super bitter. I bought this fancy electric kettle that lets you set the exact temperature for different kinds of tea, but the buttons on it are starting to stick. It's just like that old VCR remote I was talking about. Everything is made so cheap nowadays. You buy something and it breaks in a month, and then you have to throw it away because it costs more to fix it than to just buy a new one. It's terrible for the environment, all that electronic waste just sitting in landfills...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Strong beliefs? No, I don't think so. I mean, I just know what I know, you know? Like how they arrange the boxes at the warehouse, there's a right way to do it, even if the foreman doesn't get it. My mom used to say if you put a little baking soda in the boiling water, the shells slide right off, but I always forget to buy baking soda. The baking aisle at the supermarket is always so crowded anyway, especially around the holidays. Everyone suddenly decides they're a master baker in December. They buy all these bags of flour and sugar and then probably just leave them sitting in the pantry until they expire. I had a bag of flour get those little weevil bugs in it once, it was disgusting. I had to throw out everything in the cupboard...

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.9, F44.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m Dr. ____. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess they brought me in because of what happened at the warehouse. I work loading freight, and lately I keep doing things like... just freezing with my arms up right by the forklift. Or I start copying the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up, and doing all the hand signals. I was moving the boxes in these strange little patterns even when everyone was yelling for me to stop. The boxes are mostly corrugated cardboard, which is actually really bad for the environment if you don't recycle it right. I took this environmental science class for a semester at community college before I started this job, and the professor—Mr. Davis, he always wore these awful brown corduroy jackets—he said cardboard takes up way too much space in landfills. Did you know that? They use a lot of tape on our boxes too, the heavy-duty clear kind that just ruins your work gloves if you aren't careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don't... I haven't heard anyone talking like that. It's really hard to hear much of anything at work anyway because of the forklifts. The propane ones are so loud, and they smell like rotten eggs if the tank isn't sealed right. We actually had a leak last year and they made us all stand out in the parking lot for like two hours. It was freezing out there, and I only had my grey hoodie on. The zipper on it is completely busted. It always gets stuck right in the middle, so I tried rubbing candle wax on the teeth because my grandfather told me that fixes it. He used to keep a whole junk drawer full of old birthday candles in his kitchen just for fixing things. They were those little striped ones that you can never get completely blown out, you know? The trick candles. I hate those.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods when you felt unusually high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Energized? Not really, no. I mean, I'm mostly just tired from all the lifting. I usually have to drink a couple of those green energy drinks just to get through a shift. The vending machine in the breakroom is supposed to be stocked on Thursdays, but the guy who does it is always late. He drives this beat-up white van with a huge dent in the back door. It looks like somebody backed it straight into a telephone pole. Telephone poles are actually treated with this chemical, creosote, to stop them from rotting, which is why they look so dark and greasy at the bottom. My uncle tried to use old telephone poles to build a retaining wall in his garden once, but the chemicals leached into the dirt and killed all his tomatoes. He was so mad about it. He had these big heirloom ones, the purple kind that look all lumpy...\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting into trouble for deliberately breaking rules or other people’s property?\n\nPatient: Break rules? No, I don't do that. I don't break people's stuff on purpose. Sometimes things just break at work anyway, though, like the wooden pallets. They use this really cheap pine that cracks if the forklift drops it even an inch, and then it splinters everywhere. You have to be really careful because the splinters can go right through your jeans. My cousin actually got a massive splinter in his leg once when we were building a treehouse, and we had to try and dig it out with this tiny Swiss Army knife. It was one of those classic red ones, but it had those little spring scissors that don't actually cut anything? I don't know why they even bother putting those scissors on there, they just fold the paper over instead of slicing it. My dad used to have a much better knife with a real bone handle, but he lost it when we went fishing at this lake up north. The water there was so murky you couldn't even see the bottom, mostly because of all the algae blooms from the fertilizer runoff from the farms nearby. It turns the water this weird neon green color...\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Happy? Uh, no, not really. I mean, work is just work, you know? It's not exactly a party down there at the warehouse. The fluorescent lights they have in there give me a headache anyway. They hum. It's this low buzz that you don't notice at first, but then it just burrows right into your skull. They're those long tube ones, and half of them are flickering because the ballasts are going bad. My buddy tried to change one of those ballasts once in his garage and he shocked himself so hard it threw him backward into a stack of winter tires. He had this old set of snow tires for a Honda Civic he didn't even own anymore. I don't know why people hold onto stuff like that. Like, my mom has a whole closet full of Tupperware containers, but none of them have matching lids. You try to put away leftover spaghetti and you're just standing there for ten minutes trying to find a lid that actually snaps on. And the tomato sauce stains the plastic anyway, so they all look kind of orange no matter how many times you run them through the dishwasher. The dishwasher at my apartment is completely useless, it just kind of splashes warm water on the plates and calls it a day...\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble telling what is real and what isn’t?\n\nPatient: Real? I mean, yeah, I know what's real. I'm sitting right here, aren't I? The chair is real. It's got that weird fake leather on it that squeaks every time you shift your weight. My boss has a chair just like this in his office, only his is black and this one's kind of... maroon? Or burgundy maybe. I don't really know the difference. My ex-girlfriend used to paint her nails this color called \"Vampire's Kiss\" or something, and it looked just like this chair. She was always painting her nails on the couch, and one time she spilled the whole bottle of polish right on the cushion. We tried everything to get it out—nail polish remover, baking soda, club soda. Club soda is basically just carbonated water with some minerals added, right? I tried drinking it once when my stomach was upset, but it tasted terrible. It's way too salty. I'd rather just drink ginger ale if my stomach hurts. The ginger is supposed to soothe your digestion, but I'm pretty sure most of the ginger ale you buy at the store doesn't even have real ginger in it anymore, it's just artificial flavoring and high fructose corn syrup. That stuff is in everything now, even bread. You look at the ingredients on a loaf of white bread and it's like a chemistry experiment...\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt fine?\n\nPatient: Less sleep? No, I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get enough sleep, I'm just dragging all day at the warehouse. It's hard to get a good night's rest lately anyway, because my mattress has this busted spring right in the middle that pokes me in the ribs. I actually found that mattress sitting on the curb when I was moving into my apartment. Moving is the absolute worst. We had to rent a U-Haul, but the guy at the rental place gave us the wrong keys at first. He tried to give me the keys to this massive 26-foot box truck instead of the little cargo van I reserved. I don't even know how to drive a truck that big. My uncle drives an 18-wheeler, though. He hauls frozen chicken across the country, mostly down to Texas and back. He told me the refrigeration unit on his trailer is so loud he has to wear foam earplugs when he sleeps in the back of the cab. I can't stand earplugs, they always make my ears itch inside. It's like this weird wax buildup thing. The Q-tip box literally says right on the side not to put them inside your ear canal, but everybody does it anyway. What else are you even supposed to use them for? Cleaning dust out of your computer keyboard, I guess. I tried doing that with a can of compressed air once, but if you hold the can upside down it sprays this freezing cold liquid everywhere...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you became completely motionless or seemed unable to move, like staying in one position for a long time?\n\nPatient: Motionless? Well, I mean, I guess that's why they brought me in here in the first place. At the warehouse, I... I keep doing this thing where I just freeze. Like, I'll be right by the forklift and I'll just stop with my arms up in the air, and I start copying the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up. Beep. Beep. And I do the hand signals the spotters use, just over and over. Or I'll start moving the boxes, but I have to arrange them in these strange little patterns, and I just keep doing it even when the foreman and everybody is yelling at me to stop. They get so mad when you don't move the boxes fast enough. The boxes are mostly cardboard, obviously, but some of them have this weird waxy coating on them that makes them impossible to grip. If you don't wear gloves, they just slide right out of your hands. I lost my favorite pair of work gloves last week. They were leather, really nice ones my dad gave me. He bought them at a hardware store that went out of business a few years ago. It's a shame, too, because that hardware store had the best selection of screws and nails. You could buy them loose out of these little wooden bins instead of having to buy a whole plastic clamshell package just to get two screws. I hate those clamshell packages. You need a pair of scissors just to open the scissors you bought, it makes no sense. I sliced my thumb open trying to open a package of AA batteries once. Batteries are so expensive now. I remember when you could get a whole pack of them at the dollar store, but now they're like five bucks just for four of them. And they don't even last as long. I put some in my TV remote and they were dead in a month...\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, are you still aware of what’s going on around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? Yeah, I hear them yelling at me, but I just... I can't stop doing the signals. It's like I'm stuck on pause but the tape is still playing, you know? We used to have this old VCR when I was a kid, and if you paused it for too long, the screen would get all fuzzy and these tracking lines would roll up from the bottom. My brother was always trying to fix the tracking with the remote, but the buttons on that remote were so sticky. He spilled a whole glass of orange juice on it once. Pulp-free, thank god. I can't stand orange juice with pulp. It feels like you're drinking shredded paper. I tried making my own orange juice once with one of those manual juicers, the glass ones where you twist the orange half on the little ribbed cone thing. It took like six oranges just to get half a glass, and my wrist was aching for two days after. I guess that's why they charge so much for the fresh-squeezed stuff at the grocery store. The grocery store near my apartment just remodeled their produce section, and they put in these new misters that go off every ten minutes. It sounds like thunder right before they spray. They even play a little thunder sound effect over the speakers. It's kind of ridiculous, honestly. Who needs a thunderstorm sound effect just to buy a head of lettuce? And the lettuce is always soaking wet when you put it in the cart, so it gets the cardboard boxes all soggy if you don't put it in a plastic bag first...\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, can you stop yourself if you try?\n\nPatient: Stop myself? No, that's... that's the whole problem, I guess. That's why they brought me here in the first place, because I just keep doing it. I'll be standing by the forklift, and I just freeze with my arms up, and I can't stop copying the backup beeps or doing the hand signals. Or I'll be moving the boxes into those strange little patterns, and even when the foreman is screaming his head off at me to stop, I just can't. The foreman, he's got this vein that pops out on his forehead when he yells. It looks like a little blue worm under his skin. My buddy had a pet worm farm when we were kids, one of those plastic ant farm things but for earthworms. He used to feed them coffee grounds and eggshells. I don't drink coffee anymore, it makes my stomach do flips. I switched to green tea, but you have to be careful not to steep it too long or it gets super bitter. I bought this fancy electric kettle that lets you set the exact temperature for different kinds of tea, but the buttons on it are starting to stick. It's just like that old VCR remote I was talking about. Everything is made so cheap nowadays. You buy something and it breaks in a month, and then you have to throw it away because it costs more to fix it than to just buy a new one. It's terrible for the environment, all that electronic waste just sitting in landfills...\n\nDoctor: Have you had any strong beliefs that other people would say are strange or not true?\n\nPatient: Strong beliefs? No, I don't think so. I mean, I just know what I know, you know? Like how they arrange the boxes at the warehouse, there's a right way to do it, even if the foreman doesn't get it. But strange beliefs... no, nobody's ever said that to me. People say a lot of things, though. Like my neighbor, he's always going on about the city council putting something in the tap water to make the stray cats disappear. I don't know about that, but the water in my building does taste kind of metallic sometimes. I bought one of those filter pitchers you keep in the fridge, but you have to change the filter every two months and they cost a fortune. I tried buying the generic brand filters online once, but they didn't fit right and water just leaked all over the top shelf of my fridge. It ruined a whole carton of eggs. I hate wasting eggs. I usually hard-boil them for lunch, but peeling them is such a pain. Sometimes the shell sticks to the egg white and you end up tearing half the egg apart just trying to get it off. My mom used to say if you put a little baking soda in the boiling water, the shells slide right off, but I always forget to buy baking soda. The baking aisle at the supermarket is always so crowded anyway, especially around the holidays. Everyone suddenly decides they're a master baker in December. They buy all these bags of flour and sugar and then probably just leave them sitting in the pantry until they expire. I had a bag of flour get those little weevil bugs in it once, it was disgusting. I had to throw out everything in the cupboard...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

I can't stand orange juice with pulp. It feels like you're drinking shredded paper. It took like six oranges just to get half a glass, and my wrist was aching for two days after. I guess that's why they charge so much for the fresh-squeezed stuff at the grocery store. The grocery store near my apartment just remodeled their produce section, and they put in these new misters that go off every ten minutes. It sounds like thunder right before they spray. They even play a little thunder sound effect over the speakers. It's kind of ridiculous, honestly. Who needs a thunderstorm sound effect just to buy a head of lettuce? And the lettuce is always soaking wet when you put it in the cart, so it gets the cardboard boxes all soggy if you don't put it in a plastic bag first...\n\nDoctor: When you freeze like that, can you stop yourself if you try?\n\nPatient: Stop myself? No, that's... that's the whole problem, I guess. That's why they brought me here in the first place, because I just keep doing it. I'll be standing by the forklift, and I just freeze with my arms up, and I can't stop copying the backup beeps or doing the hand signals. Or I'll be moving the boxes into those strange little patterns, and even when the foreman is screaming his head off at me to stop, I just can't. The foreman, he's got this vein that pops out on his forehead when he yells. It looks like a little blue worm under his skin. My buddy had a pet worm farm when we were kids, one of those plastic ant farm things but for earthworms. He used to feed them coffee grounds and eggshells. I don't drink coffee anymore, it makes my stomach do flips. I switched to green tea, but you have to be careful not to steep it too long or it gets super bitter. I bought this fancy electric kettle that lets you set the exact temperature for different kinds of tea, but the buttons on it are starting to stick. It's just like that old VCR remote I was talking about. Everything is made so cheap nowadays. You buy something and it breaks in a month, and then you have to throw it away because it costs more to fix it than to just buy a new one. It's terrible for the environment, all that electronic waste just sitting in landfills...\n\nDoctor: Have you had any strong beliefs that other people would say are strange or not true?\n\nPatient: Strong beliefs? No, I don't think so. I mean, I just know what I know, you know? Like how they arrange the boxes at the warehouse, there's a right way to do it, even if the foreman doesn't get it. But strange beliefs... no, nobody's ever said that to me. People say a lot of things, though. Like my neighbor, he's always going on about the city council putting something in the tap water to make the stray cats disappear. I don't know about that, but the water in my building does taste kind of metallic sometimes. I bought one of those filter pitchers you keep in the fridge, but you have to change the filter every two months and they cost a fortune. I tried buying the generic brand filters online once, but they didn't fit right and water just leaked all over the top shelf of my fridge. It ruined a whole carton of eggs. I hate wasting eggs. I usually hard-boil them for lunch, but peeling them is such a pain. Sometimes the shell sticks to the egg white and you end up tearing half the egg apart just trying to get it off. My mom used to say if you put a little baking soda in the boiling water, the shells slide right off, but I always forget to buy baking soda. The baking aisle at the supermarket is always so crowded anyway, especially around the holidays. Everyone suddenly decides they're a master baker in December. They buy all these bags of flour and sugar and then probably just leave them sitting in the pantry until they expire. I had a bag of flour get those little weevil bugs in it once, it was disgusting. I had to throw out everything in the cupboard...\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you freeze like that, do you lose awareness of what’s happening around you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF44.9, F20.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m Dr. ____. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess they brought me in because of what happened at the warehouse. I work loading freight, and lately I keep doing things like... just freezing with my arms up right by the forklift. Or I start copying the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up, and doing all the hand signals. I was moving the boxes in these strange little patterns even when everyone was yelling for me to stop. The boxes are mostly corrugated cardboard, which is actually really bad for the environment if you don't recycle it right. I took this environmental science class for a semester at community college before I started this job, and the professor—Mr. Davis, he always wore these awful brown corduroy jackets—he said cardboard takes up way too much space in landfills. Did you know that? They use a lot of tape on our boxes too, the heavy-duty clear kind that just ruins your work gloves if you aren't careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don't... I haven't heard anyone talking like that. It's really hard to hear much of anything at work anyway because of the forklifts. The propane ones are so loud, and they smell like rotten eggs if the tank isn't sealed right. We actually had a leak last year and they made us all stand out in the parking lot for like two hours. It was freezing out there, and I only had my grey hoodie on. The zipper on it is completely busted. It always gets stuck right in the middle, so I tried rubbing candle wax on the teeth because my grandfather told me that fixes it. He used to keep a whole junk drawer full of old birthday candles in his kitchen just for fixing things. They were those little striped ones that you can never get completely blown out, you know? The trick candles. I hate those.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods when you felt unusually high, energized, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Energized? Not really, no. I mean, I'm mostly just tired from all the lifting. I usually have to drink a couple of those green energy drinks just to get through a shift.
